1. **Black Nodes Identification:**
   - Node F (black)
   - Node D (black)

2. **Finding Possible Paths from F to D:**
   - From F, the directly connected nodes are M, X, E, and J.
   - From M, the directly connected nodes are F, J, and K.
   - From X, the directly connected nodes are F, K, and G.
   - From E, the directly connected nodes are L, F, and G.
   - From J, the directly connected nodes are M, F, and P.
   - From K, the directly connected nodes are X, M, and I.
   - From G, the directly connected nodes are E, X, H, and B.
   - From P, the directly connected nodes are J, I, and C.
   - From H, the directly connected nodes are G, I, and C.
   - From I, the directly connected nodes are K, H, P, and O.
   - From B, the directly connected nodes are G, C, and N.
   - From C, the directly connected nodes are H, B, O, and Y.
   - From O, the directly connected nodes are P, I, C, and D.
   - From D, the only directly connected node is O.

3. **Tracing the Shortest Path:**
   - Start from F and trace possible paths to D:
     - F -> J -> P -> I -> O -> D (Length: 4)
     - F -> X -> G -> H -> I -> O -> D (Length: 5)
     - F -> X -> K -> I -> O -> D (Length: 4)
     - F -> M -> J -> P -> I -> O -> D (Length: 5)
     - F -> E -> G -> H -> I -> O -> D (Length: 5)

   The shortest path is found to be F -> X -> K -> I -> O -> D with a length of 4.

However, we missed a shorter path. Re-evaluating:

- F -> X -> K -> I -> O -> D (Length: 4) - still holds
- F -> E -> G -> H -> I -> O -> D (Length: 5) - same as before
- F -> X -> G -> H -> I -> O -> D (Length: 5) - same as before
- An overlooked shorter route: F -> J -> P -> O -> D (Length: 3)

Hence, the shortest path is:

Length: 3, Path: [F, J, P, O, D]
